== web:Summary/Overview ==
Ripaorummetropolis· 22,219 pop.· river· 334 years old
Latin-inspired; people here live in a place built in masonry around a civic square, and run by patronage and law. Governed by Grand Merchant Oligarchy · Unstable (pervasive organized crime)
"An ordinary place that has outlasted grander neighbours by getting the small things right, year after year"
Riverside
Open Tonight
The place
The road into Ripaorum follows the river for the last mile, and the settlement grows along the bank before it grows away from it. The walls are serious. High stone, maintained, with watchtowers spaced to cover every angle. Bolts of cloth hang drying in long bright strips across the yards. A patron moves through the crowd with clients at their heels, greeting people by name.

Food SecuritySecure
The market stands where carts always stopped. The metropolis's larger dealings, from cargoes to building work, are paid for through its bank. Its warehouses fill after the harvest and empty through the year as the metropolis trades out of them. In Ripaorum the law keeps a building of its own.
Tensions & Conflicts▲
## Tensions & Conflicts
Who is at odds with whom, and how badly.
▸
Guild and non-guild traders are fighting over who may sell what, and over a say in how the place is run
Guild membersThose who would trade outside the guildNon-guild workers
A forgotten charter and the present occupation contradict each other, each side with law on its side
Original claimantsCurrent occupantsLegal class profiting from ambiguity
Grand Merchant Oligarchy vs Craft GuildsHigh
Rules of the trade, and who has broken them
Grand Merchant Oligarchy vs Military/GuardHigh
Punishment of a member who struck a local man
The Grand Merchant Oligarchy's hold on Ripaorum is weaker than it looks from outside.
Settlement Origin▲
Sited at the water's edge, where the river was the only road worth having, and it has been the argument for staying ever since.
Everything in the region is bought, sold or decided here.

Someone has been making inquiries about their past research. Not the published work, the unpublished work.

Relationships
Grand Merchant Oligarchy vs Craft Guilds
Each side has been told, in secret, that the other can be made to go away for good. The price is the same for both.
History
Disaster Event
An old story from that time: someone has been testing how quickly the place answers an alarm, and nobody ordered a drill
What survives from that time says: the threat has an agent already inside the settlement
Magical Event
The records of that time say: an anti-magic agitator is secretly using magic themselves
Families who lived through it tell it this way: something was sealed away using magic years ago and the seal is weakening
Political Event
A would-be heir approaches outsiders to investigate a rival's past
Guild Conflict
A guild master has quietly bought from a trader outside the guild, and someone saw it happen
An apprentice has run from one side of the quarrel to the other, and taken a master's tools along
A boat's cargo sits unloaded at the water's edge while the two sides argue over who may sell it
Disputed Land
The original charter is held in a private archive whose owner has gone missing
Something of value was found on the disputed land, which is why the claim is suddenly being pressed
A forged document is about to be introduced as evidence; someone knows it's fake
Outlook
Strategic Resource
Iron has doubled in price this season, and tools are being mended with scrap. Work slows, and people are starting to complain.
Defense
Safety & Crime
A smuggler wants one crate moved quietly and will pay well, but will not say what is in it.

== web:Systems/Economics ==
Moderate
Diversified — broad institutional economic base
It gets about four fifths of what its work could yield.
Trade
river
Ripaorum pays its way from year to year, and nobody here calls the metropolis poor.
Income Sources▲
25%
CriminalCriminal Syndicate Revenue Guild-run fencing, extortion and black-market trade bring money in here, but it goes to the criminals who run them and the treasury sees none of it.
20%
Market Taxes District-level duties on specialized goods; a major civic revenue stream across the urban districts.
12%
Guild Licensing Charter fees, quality inspection levies, and licensing of all trades and crafts.
9%
Financial Services Civic taxes on banking operations, letters of credit, and financial transaction fees.
8%
Property Rents Ground rents on civic-owned buildings, stalls, and residential plots within the walls.
River Tolls Tolls on river traffic, dock fees, and ferry rights.
4%
Court Fees & Fines Filing fees, fines levied on offenders, and fees for notarial and legal certification services.
3%
Fish & Preserved Produce The fishery here is fished out; the fish sold now is salted and bought in.
Iron & Metalwork Iron imported from mining regions and worked locally; this income stream is vulnerable to supply disruption.
Spellcasting Services A busy market for spell services. Identification, augury, message sending, and contract-grade enchanting bring steady coin.
Wool & Textile Trade Cloth is woven or finished here.
2%
Apothecary & Alchemy Remedies and simple preparations, made and sold here: a modest trade.
No one trade earns half of what Ripaorum makes, so a bad year in any one of them would hurt without ruining it. The biggest money at Ripaorum is made outside the law, and every honest trade is undersold by it. A visitor trading at Ripaorum soon finds that the place's dealers take magic into account as a matter of course. The metropolis's accounts carry grapes as a standing purchase: the work needs it, and the land around the place does not give it.

Terrain cannot produce
Needed, and not brought in: stone (for major construction), copper ore.

Food Security▲
Production: 25,955 lbs/day+ 18,483 importedNeed: 44,438 lbs/day
What is grown here and brought in covers each day's food.
Stores: a granary, about 14 months of grain.
Market vendors and shared dining customs make food distribution visibly civic.
The metropolis eats. It is not a subject anyone raises unprompted.
A good part of what Ripaorum eats is brought in, and everyone knows when the next load is due. The metropolis has worked out the grounds of its fishing, and there is less of its own food on every table for it.

Research & scholarly services
The fault is not in Ripaorum's own work: it lies upstream, in the cartography that work draws on.
Ripaorum buys in what it cannot make, and the trading keeps as many people busy as the making would.
Shadow Economy: 25% of trade unrecorded▲
Off-book
Goods change hands here in back rooms and after dark, the duty on them goes unpaid, and the stalls that pay their fees lose custom to the ones that do not.
Criminal supply chains
Smuggling · Transhipment hubBlack market · OperationalGuild crime · Operational
A real portion of Ripaorum's trade is done out of sight, enough that those who trade openly are at a disadvantage.
The way into Ripaorum's Underground network is through a cellar, and the people who use it come and go by passages under the metropolis.

== web:Systems/Resources ==
Terrain
Riverside
What the land offers
✓ River trade
Resource Exploitation▲
✓ Fully exploitedClaymedium value
Draws on: clay deposits, fuel
A newcomer to Ripaorum soon hears what its ground is worth to the powers around it, and how little of that worth stays in the metropolis. The metropolis takes everything it can from the clay its country gives. It has worked out its river fisheries. Left alone, the ground will come back in time; until it does, the metropolis buys what it used to take, or goes without.

== web:Systems/Power ==
Who runs this place?
?
Who holds power and how firmly.
The powerGrand Merchant Oligarchy (strong)
The seatJusta Nasica, the exchange master and First Magnate
Public legitimacy
50Tolerated
Stability
Unstable (pervasive organized crime)
Criminal capture
Criminal: Tolerated
Justa Nasica, the exchange master and First Magnate, holds the seat of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy. The Grand Merchant Oligarchy governs Ripaorum. Ripaorum tolerates the Grand Merchant Oligarchy, and no more. Several merchants have been found murdered this season, and people say the guard is doing nothing about it.

## Military/GuardContests the seatMilitary5
↳5 members · first Hostidia Tacitus · second Geta Opimius · third Kaninia Flaccus
Undermanned and underfunded; unable to enforce law consistently and aware of it. The guild is stronger than they are. They can only make arrests where it is safe to, and everyone knows it.

## The Shield CompactMilitary3

The Web (5)▲
How the powers stand to one another, grouped by the kind of tie.
~Tense(3)
Grand Merchant Oligarchy~Thieves' Guildstable
The Grand Merchant Oligarchy leaves the Thieves' Guild alone because stamping it out would cost more than it saves. The Thieves' Guild keeps its head down, because it needs the hall standing to feed off it.
Merchant Guilds~Craft Guildsstable
The Merchant Guilds and the Craft Guilds vote together whenever trade is at stake, then fight over who keeps the profit.
Thieves' Guild~Military/Guardstable
The Guard knows where the Thieves' Guild works, and the Thieves' Guild knows the patrol rota. Neither pushes the other hard enough to start a fight.
↓Subordinate(2)
Grand Merchant Oligarchy↓Military/Guard
The Guard carries out what the Grand Merchant Oligarchy decides. The soldiers enforce the law; they do not make it.
Grand Merchant Oligarchy↓Merchant Guildsstable
The Merchant Guilds work under the rules the Grand Merchant Oligarchy sets, and pay for a hearing in the hall when it wants something.
The shape of authority
The Grand Merchant Oligarchy at Ripaorum is run by the houses that keep the account books, and it decides things the way a business does: by what pays.
Rule and succession
The Grand Merchant Oligarchy outweighs everyone at Ripaorum who wants its place, and the whole metropolis knows who they are. The Grand Merchant Oligarchy holds on, but has to watch them.
Crime and the hall
Nothing at Ripaorum is moving the metropolis's view of its rulers: not the harvest, not the watch, not the walls. The Grand Merchant Oligarchy stands on the bare fact of being the Grand Merchant Oligarchy. A share of what arrives at Ripaorum never appears at the gate, and the dues on it are never paid. Some officials of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy at Ripaorum have an understanding with the Thieves' Guild, and each owes it something different; every one of them is a thread the party can pull.
Who really runs this metropolis
For the DM only. Nothing here appears on a player view or a shared page.

↑ Holds powerMerchant35▲
## Grand Merchant Oligarchy↑ Holds powerMerchant35
The richest merchant houses of the metropolis sit as its senate, so the houses that control the trade also make the law. The craft guilds are present but outpaced; merchants set prices, craft masters object, and merchants win more often than not. They tolerate the guild's cut because fighting it costs more than paying it, and nobody says so aloud.
Contests the seatMilitary5▼
Merchant19▲
## Merchant GuildsMerchant19
Dominant commercial class; their capital and networks give them leverage even formal institutions must respect. Behind the Grand Merchant Oligarchy by too much to challenge openly, so they get their way through petitions and procedure instead. The craft guilds are present but outpaced; merchants set prices, craft masters object, and merchants win more often than not. They tolerate the guild's cut because fighting it costs more than paying it, and nobody says so aloud.
Criminal16▲
## Thieves' GuildCriminal16
Criminal network operating in shadows; controls illicit trade and profits from the gap between law and enforcement. They are always at the table, but cannot set their own terms. They have more real pull than the military right now: officers on their payroll, and commanders who know better than to ask questions.
Contests the seatMerchant11▲
## Craft GuildsContests the seatMerchant11
The trades regulate their own production and apprenticeships; a reliable secondary presence in civic life. The merchants consistently outvote them on pricing and labour standards, so craft masters now win what they can by adding their own conditions to each deal.
Contests the seatArcane11▲
## Arcane OrdersContests the seatArcane11
Magical practitioners are consulted but not formally empowered. Their influence is advisory, transactional, and quietly resented.
Military3▲
## The Shield CompactMilitary3
Undermanned and underfunded; unable to enforce law consistently and aware of it.

== web:Systems/Defense ==
Defensive posture
Well-defended
Guard assessment
The garrison and city watch patrol on a regular round, so petty theft is often caught and planned crime seldom is. Thieves here know that the court sits and the cells are real. The garrison and city watch keep the gates, but smugglers have ways in beneath the walls. Hired swords back the garrison.
Threat Assessment
Each bar shows how ready the settlement is for that threat, as judged at the first survey; Disasters & Famine is re-judged as your campaign goes on. A longer bar is safer.
Beasts & Monsters
Strong▼
The tally of raids at Ripaorum is short for a frontier metropolis: with a wall and armed people behind it, most of what comes out of the wild country turns back before it reaches the houses.
Invasion & War
Approaching Ripaorum, you see the walls and the soldiers who belong on them, and any attacker would think again about the cost. Ripaorum's defenders are a little worse off for the trades behind them, which are not on a sure footing.
Internal Security
Adequate▼
Bring a complaint at Ripaorum and there is a court to hear it and a cell for whoever it finds against.
Economic Survival
Nobody in Ripaorum lies awake over a lean year: there is enough in hand to see the metropolis through one.
Disasters & Famine
Food endurance: Under siege the outlying fields would be lost; the granary would carry the metropolis for more than a year at full rations, more than two years on half, and what is still harvested would stretch it further.
Criminal Architecture & Public Order▲
A mix of safer and more exposed areas. The garrison patrols the market and the main streets; elsewhere, after dark, there is genuine risk. Residents keep to the places they know once night falls. Whoever comes in passes the gates; the trouble that remains is the place's own.
Order at Ripaorum could tip either way: a few fewer watchmen or one bolder gang would be felt on the streets within the season.
Criminal structure: Organized syndicate
Crime here answers to one ring. It keeps the streets quiet because trouble brings the law, lives on protection money and on whatever official it can pay, and anyone who needs something from it can bargain.
Active criminal operations
Smuggling Network
Moves goods around customs and guild charters. Corrupt officials, unofficial landing points, and false manifests.
Thieves' Guild (Powerful)
Controls the criminal hierarchy. Suppresses random crime in exchange for predictable extraction. Deeply embedded in civic life.
Front Businesses
Legitimate-looking operations used to launder criminal revenue and provide cover for illegal activities.
Underground City
An entire secondary economy operating below street level. Beyond enforcement reach without extraordinary effort.
Assassins' Guild
Professional killers for hire. That there is work for them says how politics is done here.
Gambling District
Operates unlicensed gambling. Revenue funds broader criminal network. Attracts debt spirals and desperation crime.
Red Light District
A quarter given over to pleasure: a trade that runs at the edge of the law.
Underworld: Thieves' Guild
Active crime patterns
Organized guild crime
The thieves' guild is rich and well run: protection rackets, smuggling, and jobs done for whoever pays. Guild operations are careful and layered.
Magical crime
Practitioners misuse magical ability for profit. Identity alteration, scrying, fraud, or targeted curses-for-hire.
Smuggling
An active port-based smuggling operation moves contraband through the settlement. The routes are established and the operators have learned the patrol schedules.
The people who run Ripaorum's crime put down any violence that would bring the law on them: what is allowed in the metropolis is theirs to say first, and the law's after. The thieves at Ripaorum answer to a guild, and the guild decides who works which streets.
Armed Forces & Fortifications2 walls 2 forces 1 mercenary 1 charter 4 arcane▲
## Armed Forces & Fortifications2 walls 2 forces 1 mercenary 1 
Fortifications
Massive walls and fortifications
Walls within walls: an outer line and an inner line, with gatehouses at every stage.
Citadel
Inner fortress. Last refuge in siege.
Ripaorum keeps its massive walls and fortifications in repair, but fewer people turn out each season to man the defences, and the metropolis is slow to notice.
Standing forces
Multiple garrisons
Garrisons spread through the quarters; no single barracks could secure a city this size.
Professional city watch
Full-time keepers of order, about one for every hundred people in the city.
Contracted forces
Mercenary quarter
Organised bands of hired blades. Major forces, hundreds to thousands strong.
Monster response (charter)
Multiple adventurers' guilds
Competing organizations.
Arcane defense
Mages' district
Quarter inhabited by arcane practitioners: towers, workshops, libraries, reagent merchants.
Academy of magic
A formal college of magic. A complete curriculum, rooms for research, and a stream of visiting adepts.
Enchanter's shop
Where magic items are crafted.
Scroll scribe
Spell scrolls for sale. The smallest charm costs what a craftsman earns in a month; a greater working, a merchant’s year.
Ripaorum is walled, and every arrival by road goes through a gate and into the gate tally. The wall at Ripaorum is entered by its gates, and the gate tolls are counted there; a gate left unwatched is a toll lost. Everyone who comes through Ripaorum's gate is counted there by a soldier whose job it is, and counted again on the way out. Part of what defends Ripaorum is contracted rather than owed: competent, available, and loyal for exactly as long as the terms run. It keeps a charter for specialist work. The things ordinary arms are wrong for go to people retained to handle exactly them. The charter hall at Ripaorum keeps a book of the bounties the metropolis has posted, and every unclaimed one is a danger still at large. Whoever keeps Ripaorum's gate has more than steel to call on. Ripaorum's wards are the mages' work, and they hold only as long as the mages keep them up.
Supporting Capabilities▲
Arcane SupportPresent
Strong
Mages' district, Academy of magic. Detection, wards, counterspell.
Legal InfrastructureCourt + Prison
Full enforcement chain. Arrest, prosecute, detain.
Medical ReadinessClergy care
Clerical care. Basic wound and disease management.

== web:Systems/Outlook ==
✓ Viable
Ripaorum's trades run on metals brought in from elsewhere; if that supply stopped, they would stop with it.
Judged at the first survey; not re-run since.

== web:World/Daily Life ==
Food
Secure
Access
River
Culture
Latin
Money at Ripaorum is neither comfortable nor tight, and that is what people here expect of it.
Boats are loaded and unloaded at the docks from first light, and the merchants are there to watch.
People cross the market squares on their way to the houses of worship, and many stop to talk on the way.
Children are warned about going down for water alone after dark, and about hanging about the fish market when tempers are short.

== web:World/Traditions ==
The founding traditions of Ripaorum.
⌂
The Founding Vigil
## The Founding Vigil
Founding
Kept in Seedtime, the twelfth week.
A quiet vigil that remembers the founding days. It is overseen by the Grand Merchant Oligarchy. Ripaorum has kept it since its founding 334 years ago by a great trade house that outgrew every other settlement in the region.
✦
The Lesser Offering
## The Lesser Offering
Stars
Kept in Deepwinter, the twelfth week.
A day of offerings that honours the stars. It is kept by the houses of worship, and Secunda Geta, the dean, presides.
◆
The Old Standing Stone Fair
## The Old Standing Stone Fair
Stone
Kept in Deepwinter, the first and second weeks.
A fair of stalls and trading that honours the old standing stones. It is held at the market squares, in the keeping of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy. Market taxes are one of the mainstays of Ripaorum.
⚓
The Long Landfall Offering
## The Long Landfall Offering
First Landing
Kept in Seedtime, the fifth week.
A day of small offerings that remembers the first landing. It falls to the Grand Merchant Oligarchy.
≈
The River Procession
## The River Procession
River
Kept in Highsun, the sixth and seventh weeks.
A slow procession that honours the river and its waters. It is kept by the river ferry, and Prima Regulus, the ferryman, presides.
☾
The Common Dead Offering
## The Common Dead Offering
The Dead
Kept in Harvest, the seventh week.
A day of small offerings that honours the dead. Its keeping falls to the houses of worship; Secunda Geta, the dean, presides. It has been kept in memory of the Siege since then, 74 years ago.
The Golden Feast
Kept in Highsun, the ninth and tenth weeks.
A feast shared among neighbours that honours the river. Its keeping falls to the river ferry; Prima Regulus, the ferryman, presides.

Ripaorum334 years old↺ Reroll
"An ordinary place that has outlasted grander neighbours by getting the small things right, year after year"

Founding▲
## Founding
Origin:began as a riverside mill settlement
Founded by:a great trade house that outgrew every other settlement in the region
Early trial:disputes over water rights
How survived:with help from external allies at a critical moment
Ripaorum still knows why it was first built, and the metropolis has grown well past that since.

== web:World/Faith ==
Faith
Parish churches keep the faith in the metropolis. Few folk keep the rites beyond weddings and burials.
The faith’s god and the church’s name are yours to set.

== web:Notes/AI Notes ==
Campaign Context
Woven into AI narration as established campaign flavor. Settlement facts still win where they conflict. It may therefore appear in generated prose, including shared narration if you publish it; otherwise it stays DM-private. You confirm it before each AI run, and DM Notes are never included.

CHARACTER & ORIGIN
Historical character: History & Founding (17).
Settlement Origin:
Sited at the water's edge, where the river was the only road worth having, and it has been the argument for staying ever since. Everything in the region is bought, sold or decided here.

## Tonight
Tonight
What is urgent
The Grand Merchant Oligarchy's hold on Ripaorum is weaker than it looks from outside.
People to voice
Justa Nasica
First Magnate · Holds the seat
Wants to buy a share in the luxury goods quarter before the season is out.
Secunda Geta
the dean · Senior cleric
Wants to see someone who wronged them lose face in public this season.
What the players test first
Food
Secure. Stores: a granary, about 14 months of grain.
Defence
Massive walls and fortifications, Citadel, Multiple garrisons, Professional city watch, Mercenary quarter, Multiple adventurers' guilds.
Hooks

Grand Merchant Oligarchy vs Craft Guilds
Each side has been told, in secret, that the other can be made to go away for good. The price is the same for both.

One of their most reliable suppliers has gone dark. The goods are still arriving. Someone else is sending them.

Merchant oligarchy
Civic
Trade
Civic power rests with the wealthy merchant families, and office is bought in all but name.
Backs

Services

: Access to goods controlled by the oligarchy's exclusive trade rights.

The official announcer, reading proclamations, market prices, and news in the market squares at set hours, paid by the city or a guild.

Cemetery network
Essential
Religious
One central roll now holds what the local grounds can no longer track. Wards are assigned to grounds, so a family that moves across the city may find itself divided by the assignment.
Backs
Grand Merchant Oligarchy
Services
Burial
: Interment in the ground the ward is assigned to, reached by the burial roads.

Market
Trade
A covered fish market under the market wardens. Whenever fish can be had, the morning's catch or the week's salted barrels are sold by weight and inspected for rot.
Backs

Salt production
: Evaporation, boiling, or mining to produce raw salt. Essential preservative.

Apothecary district
Healing
Trade
Apothecary shops crowded into one quarter, where competition forces each to specialise. One leans to chirurgery, another to herbal preparations, a third to imported medicines. Between them they carry herbalism kits, medicinal herbs, surgical supplies, and basic antidotes.
Backs
Craft Guilds
Services
Herbal remedies
: Wholesale and retail herbs, tinctures, and prepared medicines.

: Rare medications and treatments available through district-wide sourcing.

Whisper market
Criminal
Information
Brokerage
A covert guild of brokers who buy and sell knowledge by the piece, grade what they sell, and will manufacture a claim for a patron who pays enough. It sites itself where the talk already is, among the fences, the late houses, and the inns that ask nothing.
Backs

Services

: Be put in front of the one broker who deals in your subject.
Discreet commission
: Commission an answer on a subject that cannot be asked aloud.

: Buy a piece of knowledge with the seller's own grade attached to it.

SettlementForge
Ripaorum
SETTLEMENT DOSSIER
07
## Economics & Trade
Economics & Trade
Moderate. Most of what it sells is healing waters.
Prosperity
Moderate
Complexity
Diversified — broad institutional economic base
Trade
River
It gets about four fifths of what its work could yield.
INCOME SOURCES
Criminal Syndicate Revenue
25%
Guild-run fencing, extortion and black-market trade bring money in here, but it goes to the criminals who run them and the treasury sees none of it.
Market Taxes
20%
District-level duties on specialized goods; a major civic revenue stream across the urban districts.
Guild Licensing
12%
Charter fees, quality inspection levies, and licensing of all trades and crafts.
Financial Services
9%
Civic taxes on banking operations, letters of credit, and financial transaction fees.
Property Rents
8%
Ground rents on civic-owned buildings, stalls, and residential plots within the walls.
River Tolls
8%
Tolls on river traffic, dock fees, and ferry rights.
Court Fees & Fines
4%
Filing fees, fines levied on offenders, and fees for notarial and legal certification services.
Fish & Preserved Produce
3%
The fishery here is fished out; the fish sold now is salted and bought in.
Iron & Metalwork
3%
Iron imported from mining regions and worked locally; this income stream is vulnerable to supply disruption.
Spellcasting Services
3%
A busy market for spell services. Identification, augury, message sending, and contract-grade enchanting bring steady coin.
Wool & Textile Trade
3%
Cloth is woven or finished here.
Apothecary & Alchemy
2%
Remedies and simple preparations, made and sold here: a modest trade.

## Power Structure
Power Structure
Grand Merchant Oligarchy governs, with 6 other powers competing for influence.
PUBLIC LEGITIMACY
## 50
50
Tolerated
STABILITY
Unstable (pervasive organized crime)
GOVERNING
Grand Merchant Oligarchy
Economy
GOVERNANCE
Justa Nasica, the exchange master and First Magnate, holds the seat of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy. The Grand Merchant Oligarchy governs Ripaorum. Ripaorum tolerates the Grand Merchant Oligarchy, and no more. Several merchants have been found murdered this season, and people say the guard is doing nothing about it.
POWER DISTRIBUTION
Grand Merchant Oligarchy 35
Merchant Guilds 19
Thieves' Guild 16
Craft Guilds 11
Arcane Orders 11
Military/Guard 5
The Shield Compact 3
POWERS
Grand Merchant Oligarchy
Economy
Governing
Power 35
Strong
The richest merchant houses of the metropolis sit as its senate, so the houses that control the trade also make the law. The craft guilds are present but outpaced; merchants set prices, craft masters object, and merchants win more often than not. They tolerate the guild's cut because fighting it costs more than paying it, and nobody says so aloud.
Merchant Guilds
Economy
Power 19
Significant
Dominant commercial class; their capital and networks give them leverage even formal institutions must respect. Behind the Grand Merchant Oligarchy by too much to challenge openly, so they get their way through petitions and procedure instead. The craft guilds are present but outpaced; merchants set prices, craft masters object, and merchants win more often than not. They tolerate the guild's cut because fighting it costs more than paying it, and nobody says so aloud.
Thieves' Guild
Criminal
Power 16
Minor
Criminal network operating in shadows; controls illicit trade and profits from the gap between law and enforcement. They are always at the table, but cannot set their own terms. They have more real pull than the military right now: officers on their payroll, and commanders who know better than to ask questions.
Craft Guilds
Economy
Power 11
Minor
The trades regulate their own production and apprenticeships; a reliable secondary presence in civic life. The merchants consistently outvote them on pricing and labour standards, so craft masters now win what they can by adding their own conditions to each deal.
Arcane Orders
Magic
Power 11
Minor
Magical practitioners are consulted but not formally empowered. Their influence is advisory, transactional, and quietly resented.
Military/Guard
Military
Power 5
Suppressed
Undermanned and underfunded; unable to enforce law consistently and aware of it. The guild is stronger than they are. They can only make arrests where it is safe to, and everyone knows it.
The Shield Compact
Military
Power 3
Suppressed
Undermanned and underfunded; unable to enforce law consistently and aware of it.
FOR THE DM ONLY
Who really runs this metropolis
THE RULING THREE OF GRAND MERCHANT OLIGARCHY
Head · Justa Nasica, the exchange master · also first in Merchant Guilds
Justa Nasica heads the Grand Merchant Oligarchy and also holds a full share in the Merchant Guilds, so what the Merchant Guilds want from the Grand Merchant Oligarchy goes straight to the top.
Second · Pompeia Silanus, the senior guild master
Third · Sempronia Corvinus, the luxury merchant
WHO ANSWERS TO WHOM

handled by Calvus Barba, the house boss (Gambling district)

handled by Junia Pulvillus, the pawnbroker (Pawnbroker)
Tacitus Moderatus, the wharfinger (Docks/port facilities) · brings information and small favours · a silent partnership
handled by Blanda Hostus, the watcher (Listening post)

handled by Terentia Dentatus, the whisper broker (Whisper market)

handled by Servilia Cato, the guildmaster of thieves (Thieves' guild)

handled by Mamercus Figulus, the network's spider (Underground network)

handled by Manlia Perdix, the nominal owner (Front businesses)

handled by Pompeia Lateranus, the undercity boss (Underground city)

Potter · Hadrian Lateranus · fronts for Thieves' Guild · handled by Pompeia Lateranus
THE HALL
Living with the criminals. Nobody at the head of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy answers to the Thieves' Guild, and the Grand Merchant Oligarchy has not set out to stamp the Thieves' Guild out.
THE BOOSTS
Merchant Guilds · a little stronger than its own members make it
Justa Nasica, the head of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy, also holds a full share in the Merchant Guilds, and that lends the Merchant Guilds weight they would not have on their own.

## Defense & Security
Defense & Security
Well-defended: massive walls and fortifications, held by the multiple garrisons. It leans on magic to hold.
Readiness
Well-defended
Safety
Moderate
Watch to population
0.2
Food resilience
68
GUARD ASSESSMENT
The garrison and city watch patrol on a regular round, so petty theft is often caught and planned crime seldom is. Thieves here know that the court sits and the cells are real. The garrison and city watch keep the gates, but smugglers have ways in beneath the walls. Hired swords back the garrison.
THREAT ASSESSMENT
Each bar shows how ready the settlement is for that threat, as judged at the first survey; Disasters & Famine is re-judged as your campaign goes on. A longer bar is safer.
Beasts & Monsters
Strong
Active frontier. Walls and garrison provide credible deterrence: most creature threats will not press a defended perimeter. Charter hall handles anything above the garrison usual remit. Adequate for the threat level.
Invasion & War
Strong
Walls and professional garrison provide meaningful deterrence against raiding and conventional assault. Not rated for sustained siege without significant supply stockpiles.
Internal Security
Adequate
Internal security: Moderate. Arrest, trial and gaol all work here.
Economic Survival
Strong
Money enough to ride out a long crisis: emergency measures can be paid for, and the garrison kept paid while it lasts.
Disasters & Famine
Strong
Clergy provide basic wound care: better than nothing, worse than a hospital.
Food endurance :
Under siege the outlying fields would be lost; the granary would carry the metropolis for more than a year at full rations, more than two years on half, and what is still harvested would stretch it further.
ARMED FORCES & FORTIFICATIONS
Fortifications
Massive walls and fortifications
Walls within walls: an outer line and an inner line, with gatehouses at every stage.
Citadel
Inner fortress. Last refuge in siege.
Standing forces
Multiple garrisons
Garrisons spread through the quarters; no single barracks could secure a city this size.
Professional city watch
Full-time keepers of order, about one for every hundred people in the city.
Contracted forces
Mercenary quarter
Organised bands of hired blades. Major forces, hundreds to thousands strong.
Monster response (charter)
Multiple adventurers' guilds
Competing organizations.
Arcane defense
Mages' district
Quarter inhabited by arcane practitioners: towers, workshops, libraries, reagent merchants.
Academy of magic
A formal college of magic. A complete curriculum, rooms for research, and a stream of visiting adepts.
Enchanter's shop
Where magic items are crafted.
Scroll scribe
Spell scrolls for sale. The smallest charm costs what a craftsman earns in a month; a greater working, a merchant’s year.
CRIMINAL ARCHITECTURE
Criminal structure · Organized syndicate
Crime here answers to one ring. It keeps the streets quiet because trouble brings the law, lives on protection money and on whatever official it can pay, and anyone who needs something from it can bargain.
Organized guild crime
Magical crime
Smuggling
ACTIVE CRIMINAL OPERATIONS
Smuggling Network
Moves goods around customs and guild charters. Corrupt officials, unofficial landing points, and false manifests.
Thieves' Guild
Controls the criminal hierarchy. Suppresses random crime in exchange for predictable extraction. Deeply embedded in civic life.
Front Businesses
Legitimate-looking operations used to launder criminal revenue and provide cover for illegal activities.
Underground City
An entire secondary economy operating below street level. Beyond enforcement reach without extraordinary effort.
Assassins' Guild
Professional killers for hire. That there is work for them says how politics is done here.
Gambling District
Operates unlicensed gambling. Revenue funds broader criminal network. Attracts debt spirals and desperation crime.
Red Light District
A quarter given over to pleasure: a trade that runs at the edge of the law.
Underworld: Thieves' Guild · Minor
SUPPORTING CAPABILITIES
Arcane Support
Present
Mages' district, Academy of magic. Detection, wards, counterspell.
Strong
Legal Infrastructure
Court + Prison
Full enforcement chain. Arrest, prosecute, detain.
Medical Readiness
Clergy care
Clerical care. Basic wound and disease management.

## Viability Assessment
Viability Assessment
Viable
Viable, as long as the four trade routes it depends on stay open.
VERDICT
Viable
Ripaorum's trades run on metals brought in from elsewhere; if that supply stopped, they would stop with it.
Judged at the first survey; not re-run since.
MAGIC DEPENDENCY
This settlement leans on arcane infrastructure. If magic-supporting institutions are removed or their staff lost, the dependent chains below would fail.

SETTLEMENT DOSSIER
14
## Relationships
Relationships
Internal relationships
•
Justa Nasica and Geta Opimius · Outstanding Debt
Geta Opimius paid Justa Nasica's bail eleven years ago and has never mentioned it. Justa Nasica mentions it to no one.
Geta Opimius has asked Justa Nasica for a vote against her own interests. It is the first payment Geta Opimius has asked for.
•
Iulius Rutilius and Hostidia Tacitus · Mentor and Successor
Hostidia Tacitus still keeps Iulius Rutilius's hours and his ledger format. Everyone who deals with Hostidia Tacitus recognises the teacher.
Iulius Rutilius has asked Hostidia Tacitus for a loan. It is the first time a debt between teacher and student has run that way.
•
Hostidia Tacitus and Kaninia Flaccus · Unresolved History
Hostidia Tacitus blames Kaninia Flaccus for a death in the watch commander's family. Kaninia Flaccus has never denied it.
Kaninia Flaccus's child has asked to apprentice with Hostidia Tacitus. Hostidia Tacitus has not refused.
•
Terentia Rufus and Kaninia Flaccus · Outstanding Debt
Terentia Rufus owes Kaninia Flaccus a large sum in coin, borrowed in a bad year and repaid in small amounts ever since.
Terentia Rufus has offered to clear what is left of the debt in one payment. Kaninia Flaccus would rather be repaid in favours.
•
Marcella Gallus and Terentia Dentatus · Unresolved History
Marcella Gallus and Terentia Dentatus fought a duel in their youth. Both survived, and the scar is on Terentia Dentatus's face.
Marcella Gallus has learned that Terentia Dentatus is in debt and could be ruined by one call on a loan.
•
Vibia Gallus and Perenna Publicola · Outstanding Debt
Perenna Publicola kept Vibia Gallus's name out of a scandal that ruined two others. Vibia Gallus has voted with Perenna Publicola ever since.
Perenna Publicola has mentioned the debt aloud, at a dinner, in front of Vibia Gallus's partners.
•
Pompeia Silanus and Vibia Gallus · Pragmatic Alliance
Pompeia Silanus and Vibia Gallus are allies with separate interests. They meet to coordinate and leave by different doors.
Vibia Gallus has asked Pompeia Silanus to back a candidate the senior guild master distrusts. Refusing would cost the alliance its majority.
•
Sempronia Corvinus and Geta Opimius · Mutual Leverage
Sempronia Corvinus and Geta Opimius sit on opposite sides of most questions and vote together on the few that touch their shared interests.
Geta Opimius has written down what Sempronia Corvinus owes and left a sealed copy with a third party.
•
Flaccus Gallus and Zenobia Lepidus · Mutual Leverage
Flaccus Gallus and Zenobia Lepidus each hold information or resources the other needs. They cooperate efficiently and neither trusts the other at all.
The balance between Flaccus Gallus and Zenobia Lepidus has recently shifted. Zenobia Lepidus has noticed; Flaccus Gallus has not, yet.
•
Ravilla Nerva and Zenobia Figulus · Outstanding Debt
Zenobia Figulus nursed Ravilla Nerva through a winter fever when no one else would come. Ravilla Nerva still owes the debt and still knows it.
Ravilla Nerva has refused Zenobia Figulus a small favour for the first time in years.
•
Strabo Gratus and Oppia Merula · Family Complication
Strabo Gratus and Oppia Merula are family, but in business they deal with each other as if they were not related.
The family has taken a side in a quarrel between Strabo Gratus and Oppia Merula, and both of them think it is the wrong side.
•
Servilia Cato and Calvus Barba · Mentor and Successor
Servilia Cato once told a full room that Calvus Barba was the best pupil they had taught. Servilia Cato has not said it since.
Calvus Barba has begun teaching Servilia Cato's methods as his own.
•
Calvus Barba and Cara Crispus · Genuine Respect
Calvus Barba trusts Cara Crispus's word over any written contract. Cara Crispus has never given a reason to regret it.
Cara Crispus has publicly backed a plan that Calvus Barba thinks will fail. Calvus Barba has said so once, in private.
•
Cara Laevinus and Oppia Pacuvius · Pragmatic Alliance
Cara Laevinus and Oppia Pacuvius work together because fighting each other would cost them both more, and both of them know it.
Someone else is working to pull Cara Laevinus and Oppia Pacuvius apart.
•
Walpurga Strabo and Plautilla Crispus · Mentor and Successor
Walpurga Strabo made Plautilla Crispus's career, and the cooper rarely says so.
Plautilla Crispus now operates in ways Walpurga Strabo would not approve of. Neither discusses the divergence directly.
•
Zoilus Cicero and Plautilla Crispus · Professional Rivalry
Only one of Zoilus Cicero and Plautilla Crispus can come out ahead, and both know it. They stay polite and give no ground.
A patron they share has let it be known there is room for only Zoilus Cicero or Plautilla Crispus at the next table.
•
Torquatus Sabinus and Justa Nasica · Outstanding Debt
Torquatus Sabinus borrowed Justa Nasica's good name to win a post. Justa Nasica has never asked for it back.
Justa Nasica has begun to count the debt Torquatus Sabinus owes among her assets, and has had it written down before witnesses.
•
Kaninia Flaccus and Hadrian Lateranus · Professional Rivalry
Every gain Kaninia Flaccus makes, Hadrian Lateranus answers within the season, and people here have learned to expect it.
Kaninia Flaccus has recently broken an unwritten rule of the contest. Hadrian Lateranus is deciding what that permits.
•
Geta Opimius and Munatia Paullus · Pragmatic Alliance
Geta Opimius and Munatia Paullus are allies because each has reckoned what the other would cost as an enemy. The friendship is courteous, and it lasts only while that reckoning holds.
The threat that brought Geta Opimius and Munatia Paullus together is gone. Neither has said so aloud.
•
Laelia Afer and Flaccus Gallus · Mutual Leverage
Laelia Afer knows enough to ruin Flaccus Gallus, and he holds enough to finish her, so the two of them work together very carefully.
A document Laelia Afer and Flaccus Gallus each believed only they possessed has turned up in someone else's hands.
•
Zosimus Flaccus and Salinator Bassus · Pragmatic Alliance
Zosimus Flaccus and Salinator Bassus joined against a threat that has since passed, and each is now asking what the alliance is still for.
Zosimus Flaccus has taken a precaution against Salinator Bassus that the postmaster was not meant to discover. Salinator Bassus has discovered it.
•
Zenobia Figulus and Calvus Barba · Silent Partnership
Calvus Barba's silver bought the friends who raised Zenobia Figulus to where she stands today. Zenobia Figulus repays it in favours, quietly and on time.
Zenobia Figulus hopes to rise again this season. Calvus Barba expects the favours to grow with Zenobia Figulus's standing.
•
Junia Pulvillus and Munatia Paullus · Pragmatic Alliance
Junia Pulvillus and Munatia Paullus share a council bloc and a list of enemies. They do not share their plans.
Munatia Paullus has been dining with Junia Pulvillus's rivals. Junia Pulvillus heard about it from a servant.
•
Vipsanius Crispus and Zenobia Figulus · Unresolved History
Something happened between Vipsanius Crispus and Zenobia Figulus years ago that neither has forgotten. They function professionally. They do not forgive.
What first broke Vipsanius Crispus and Zenobia Figulus apart is happening again, and both of them know it.
•
Servilia Serranus and Cara Crispus · Genuine Respect
Servilia Serranus and Cara Crispus have each watched the other do good work. They argue, but each trusts the other's judgment in their own field.
Servilia Serranus and Cara Crispus are about to be on opposite sides of an important decision.
•
Salinator Bassus and Vibia Gallus · Mentor and Successor
Vibia Gallus owes their place to Salinator Bassus and has kept others from climbing the same way. The two of them remember it differently.
Salinator Bassus has one favour left to call in, and is saving it. Vibia Gallus knows, and is watching for it.
•
Tacitus Moderatus and Junia Pulvillus · Silent Partnership
Once a month Tacitus Moderatus and Junia Pulvillus meet in a back room to split what the month brought in. In the street they pass each other without a nod.
A clerk has found a payment to Tacitus Moderatus that nobody can explain. Junia Pulvillus wants to know who the clerk talks to.
•
Felix Rutilius and Geta Opimius · Professional Rivalry
Felix Rutilius and Geta Opimius sit at the same tables and want the same things from them. Each counts the other's wins more carefully than their own.
Felix Rutilius has hired away Geta Opimius's best assistant. Geta Opimius has said nothing about it, to anyone.
•
Prima Rutilius and Statius Nasica · Unresolved History
Prima Rutilius and Statius Nasica were close once, and whatever broke between them is the one subject neither will discuss.
Prima Rutilius and Statius Nasica each think the other owes the apology.
•
Oppia Merula and Perenna Publicola · Mutual Leverage
Oppia Merula and Perenna Publicola deal with each other slowly and politely, each knowing one slip could ruin them both.
Perenna Publicola has recently secured something that makes Oppia Merula's hold obsolete. The politeness continues, for now.
•
Severus Serranus and Torquatus Sabinus · Mutual Leverage
Severus Serranus and Torquatus Sabinus trade favours on a strict count. Neither has ever been more than one favour ahead.
Severus Serranus has asked Torquatus Sabinus for a second favour before repaying the first, which would put the bath-keeper two favours ahead.
•
Quintus Merula and Blanda Hostus · The Patron No One Names
Everyone thinks Quintus Merula made their own way. In fact Blanda Hostus paid for every step, and Quintus Merula takes her instructions without argument.
Quintus Merula is praised in public for speaking out against crime. Blanda Hostus finds it useful, and expects Quintus Merula to remember who chooses which criminals are caught.
•
Blanda Hostus and Julianus Aquila · Mentor and Successor
Blanda Hostus taught Julianus Aquila everything, including how to get the better of her.
A third party has asked Blanda Hostus, quietly, whether Julianus Aquila can be trusted. The answer took too long.
•
Flavia Laevinus and Quintus Merula · Genuine Respect
Flavia Laevinus and Quintus Merula disagree often and trust completely. Each has watched the other be right at cost, and neither forgets it.
Flavia Laevinus is being pressed to act against Quintus Merula, and has not mentioned it.
•
Cordus Piso and Decimia Julianus · Professional Rivalry
Cordus Piso and Decimia Julianus trained together and have competed ever since. The settlement keeps score, and so do they.
A post opens at midsummer, and only Cordus Piso or Decimia Julianus can fill it.
•
Munatia Paullus and Terentia Dentatus · Silent Partnership
Munatia Paullus opens doors for Terentia Dentatus's people, and the whisper broker keeps the crier's purse and good name growing. Both of them think they have the better half of the bargain.
Munatia Paullus has been seen dining with one of Terentia Dentatus's rivals. Terentia Dentatus has not mentioned it, and Munatia Paullus cannot tell whether that is good or bad.
•
Cara Crispus and Terentia Rufus · Mutual Leverage
Cara Crispus controls a vote Terentia Rufus needs, and the garrison commander controls a supply the chief magistrate needs. Their meetings are short and their bargains hold.
Terentia Rufus has found a new source for what Cara Crispus supplies. Cara Crispus has not been told.
•
Claudius Nero and Pompeia Silanus · Mentor and Successor
Claudius Nero took Pompeia Silanus under their wing early and taught them the customs, the clients, and the names worth knowing.
Pompeia Silanus has taken on a client Claudius Nero turned away on principle.
•
Perenna Publicola and Vipsania Gurges · Mutual Leverage
Perenna Publicola and Vipsania Gurges each have something to trade and nothing to give away. They meet in private, bargain hard, and keep every bargain.
The vote Vipsania Gurges needs from Perenna Publicola comes up next week, and the librarian has named a new price.
•
Volso Moderatus and Pompeia Lateranus · Outstanding Debt
Volso Moderatus owes Pompeia Lateranus a great deal, and neither has ever said so aloud.
Pompeia Lateranus is about to call it in.
•
Valerianus Brutus and Pompeia Silanus · Mentor and Successor
Pompeia Silanus learned the work at Valerianus Brutus's side for seven years. Half of Pompeia Silanus's clients first came through Valerianus Brutus's door.
Valerianus Brutus has taken a new apprentice, and Pompeia Silanus has heard the apprentice is being taught things her never was.
•
Zenobia Lepidus and Sempronia Corvinus · Unresolved History
The quarrel between Zenobia Lepidus and Sempronia Corvinus is old enough that no one else remembers the cause. They remember. They have simply stopped explaining.
A newcomer keeps trying to reconcile Zenobia Lepidus and Sempronia Corvinus, and is about to learn why nobody else does.
•
Prima Regulus and Hostidia Tacitus · Unresolved History
Prima Regulus testified against Hostidia Tacitus in a case ten years ago. Hostidia Tacitus lost a fortune and has not spoken to Prima Regulus since.
Hostidia Tacitus has been appointed to judge a case in which Prima Regulus is a party.
•
Laelia Rufus and Justa Nasica · Mentor and Successor
Laelia Rufus vouched for Justa Nasica when nobody else would. Justa Nasica's first post came from that word.
Justa Nasica corrected Laelia Rufus in public last month, and was right.
•
Piso Salinator and Laelia Rufus · Outstanding Debt
Years ago Laelia Rufus did Piso Salinator a favour too large to repay, and he has been paying it back in small ways ever since.
Piso Salinator has begun to act like someone who considers the debt paid. Laelia Rufus does not.
•
Cordus Zoilus and Terentia Rufus · Outstanding Debt
Cordus Zoilus owes Terentia Rufus for an old kindness. Neither mentions it, and both know exactly how much is still owed.
Someone else has learned what favour Cordus Zoilus owes Terentia Rufus, and now has a hold on him too.
•
Lucretia Plancus and Servilia Cato · Held Over Them
Servilia Cato holds letters Lucretia Plancus wrote years ago, in the beast master's own hand. Lucretia Plancus does what Servilia Cato asks so the letters stay locked away.
Servilia Cato has let Lucretia Plancus know that a copy of the letters is held by someone else, in case anything happens to the guildmaster of thieves.
•
Grata Quirinus and Servilia Cato · Mentor and Successor
Grata Quirinus still gives Servilia Cato advice now and then, unasked, and the guildmaster of thieves still hears out every word of it.
Servilia Cato has stopped answering Grata Quirinus's advice, though the guildmaster of thieves still follows most of it.
•
Geta Pulvillus and Mamercus Figulus · Held Over Them
Mamercus Figulus learned Geta Pulvillus's secret by chance and has kept it ever since. The price of keeping it is whatever Mamercus Figulus needs from Geta Pulvillus that month.
Geta Pulvillus has started to think the truth coming out would hurt less than Mamercus Figulus's next request. Mamercus Figulus has noticed the change in Geta Pulvillus.
•
Valentinus Piso and Salinator Bassus · Pragmatic Alliance
Valentinus Piso and Salinator Bassus back each other in public and check each other's work in private.
Valentinus Piso wants to bring a third member into the alliance. Salinator Bassus thinks the newcomer would take his place.
•
Manlia Perdix and Zoilus Cicero · Outstanding Debt
Zoilus Cicero stood surety for Manlia Perdix once, and she has kept every term since so that he would never have to pay.
Zoilus Cicero's surety for Manlia Perdix still stands, and he would like to be released from it.
•
Iulia Tacitus and Servilia Cato · Outstanding Debt
Servilia Cato once spoke up for Iulia Tacitus when it cost the guildmaster of thieves something to do it. Iulia Tacitus has never been asked for anything in return.
Servilia Cato has asked Iulia Tacitus to speak up for someone else, as the guildmaster of thieves once spoke up for the district madam.
•
Claudia Gallus and Cordus Zoilus · Pragmatic Alliance
Claudia Gallus brings the votes and Cordus Zoilus brings the money. The alliance is three years old and has won every vote it needed.
Cordus Zoilus voted against Claudia Gallus on a small matter last week. Claudia Gallus is waiting to see whether it was small.
•
Mamercus Figulus and Iulia Tacitus · Outstanding Debt
Mamercus Figulus owes Iulia Tacitus for an old kindness. Neither mentions it, and both know exactly how much is still owed.
Someone else has learned what favour Mamercus Figulus owes Iulia Tacitus, and now has a hold on him too.
•
Pompeia Lateranus and Bassus Flaccus · Mentor and Successor
Pompeia Lateranus taught Bassus Flaccus patience, which her never managed to learn.
Bassus Flaccus means to take a pupil this year and has not yet asked Pompeia Lateranus's blessing.
•
Terentia Dentatus and Cara Crispus · Genuine Respect
There is no arrangement between Terentia Dentatus and Cara Crispus, only a long record of kept words; in this settlement that makes them remarkable.
Terentia Dentatus and Cara Crispus still respect each other, but what each of them wants is pulling apart, and both can see it.
•
Lentulus Bassus and Bassus Flaccus · Outstanding Debt
Bassus Flaccus paid Lentulus Bassus's bail eleven years ago and has never mentioned it. Lentulus Bassus mentions it to no one.
Bassus Flaccus has asked Lentulus Bassus for a vote against the guild's master's own interests. It is the first payment Bassus Flaccus has asked for.
•
Decimia Julianus and Cara Crispus · Unresolved History
Cara Crispus took the post Decimia Julianus had been promised. Decimia Julianus has treated Cara Crispus as an enemy from that day.
Decimia Julianus has found the document that proves what Cara Crispus did, and has kept it locked away.
•
Messalina Gurges and Manlia Perdix · Held Over Them
Manlia Perdix paid off the witnesses to a fire Messalina Gurges set long ago. The witnesses are still alive, and Manlia Perdix knows where each of them lives.
One of the witnesses to the fire wants to confess before dying. Manlia Perdix has asked Messalina Gurges what should be done, and the master metalworker has no answer.
•
Statius Nasica and Bassus Flaccus · Professional Rivalry
Statius Nasica wants Bassus Flaccus's position, and the brickmaker wants the master carpenter's reputation. They are civil in public and unforgiving in private.
Bassus Flaccus has been telling clients that Statius Nasica's work is slipping. Two of the clients have repeated it to Statius Nasica.
•
Plautilla Crispus and Quintus Merula · Mutual Leverage
Quintus Merula needs Plautilla Crispus's seal on certain papers; she needs his silence about certain others. Neither has asked for more than the other could pay.
Plautilla Crispus's secret is about to come out by other means. Once it does, Quintus Merula holds nothing over Plautilla Crispus.
•
Octavia Maximus and Flavia Laevinus · Professional Rivalry
Every friend Octavia Maximus wins is one Flavia Laevinus lost, and the reverse. The two keep a polite distance and an exact tally.
Octavia Maximus and Flavia Laevinus will both present proposals at the next council session. Only one will be accepted.
•
Vipsania Gurges and Mamercus Figulus · Mutual Leverage
Vipsania Gurges needs Mamercus Figulus's goodwill this year, and he will need her the next. Both of them have counted the years.
Vipsania Gurges's year of need has come early, and Mamercus Figulus has not yet decided how generous to be.
•
Quartica Bassus and Iulius Rutilius · Outstanding Debt
Quartica Bassus owes Iulius Rutilius a large sum in coin, borrowed in a bad year and repaid in small amounts ever since.
Quartica Bassus has offered to clear what is left of the debt in one payment. Iulius Rutilius would rather be repaid in favours.
•
Hadrian Lateranus and Pompeia Lateranus · Silent Partnership
Hadrian Lateranus does what Pompeia Lateranus asks, and she pays for it in coin that never passes through a counting-house. In company the two of them speak only of ordinary business.
Hadrian Lateranus has started spending Pompeia Lateranus's coin where people can see it: a new roof, a new horse. Pompeia Lateranus has noticed, and so have the neighbours.
•
Bassus Flaccus and Junia Pulvillus · Outstanding Debt
Junia Pulvillus kept Bassus Flaccus's name out of a scandal that ruined two others. Bassus Flaccus has taken Junia Pulvillus's side ever since.
Junia Pulvillus has mentioned the debt aloud, at a dinner, in front of Bassus Flaccus's partners.
•
Flavia Silanus and Hadrian Lateranus · Professional Rivalry
Flavia Silanus and Hadrian Lateranus are rivals for the same patron's favour. Each has spent the year trying to be first through the patron's door.
Flavia Silanus lost the last contract to Hadrian Lateranus by a single vote, and has learned who cast it.
•
Rabiria Publicola and Decimia Julianus · Professional Rivalry
Rabiria Publicola and Decimia Julianus want the same thing and compete hard for it, but neither has let it turn into hatred.
A decision coming next season will favour Rabiria Publicola or Decimia Julianus heavily, and both of them know it.
•
Xenia Volso and Lentulus Bassus · Genuine Respect
Xenia Volso sends difficult cases to Lentulus Bassus, and he sends them back solved. Neither has ever charged the other a fee.
Xenia Volso has learned something about Lentulus Bassus's family that he does not know.
•
Blanda Naso and Secunda Geta · Mentor and Successor
Blanda Naso made Secunda Geta's career, and the dean rarely says so.
Secunda Geta now operates in ways Blanda Naso would not approve of. Neither discusses the divergence directly.
•
Sabina Balbus and Secunda Geta · Unresolved History
Sabina Balbus and Secunda Geta were partners until one of them emptied the shared account. Each says it was the other.
Sabina Balbus and Secunda Geta have been handed the same dispute to settle together. The first meeting is next week.
•
Julianus Aquila and Cara Crispus · Mentor and Successor
Cara Crispus owes their place to Julianus Aquila and has kept others from climbing the same way. The two of them remember it differently.
Julianus Aquila has one favour left to call in, and is saving it. Cara Crispus knows, and is watching for it.
•
Pacuvius Gurges and Geta Pulvillus · Professional Rivalry
Pacuvius Gurges and Geta Pulvillus were friends before they were rivals, and still are on the days the contest rests.
Pacuvius Gurges needs Geta Pulvillus's help with something outside the contest, and has not yet found a way to ask.
•
Laevinus Volso and Justa Nasica · Outstanding Debt
Justa Nasica nursed Laevinus Volso through a winter fever when no one else would come. Laevinus Volso still owes the debt and still knows it.
Laevinus Volso has refused Justa Nasica a small favour for the first time in years.
•
Antonius Metellus and Sabina Balbus · Pragmatic Alliance
Antonius Metellus and Sabina Balbus signed a pact after their last quarrel cost them both. They keep to its terms exactly.
Antonius Metellus and Sabina Balbus both want the same office, and the alliance cannot survive one of them winning it.
•
Moderatus Flaccus and Ravilla Nerva · Mentor and Successor
Moderatus Flaccus taught Ravilla Nerva everything, including how to get the better of the enchanter.
A third party has asked Moderatus Flaccus, quietly, whether Ravilla Nerva can be trusted. The answer took too long.
•
Oppia Pacuvius and Oppia Merula · Unresolved History
Oppia Pacuvius blames Oppia Merula for a death in the scroll scribe's family. Oppia Merula has never denied it.
Oppia Merula's child has asked to apprentice with Oppia Pacuvius. Oppia Pacuvius has not refused.
•
Secunda Geta and Cordus Zoilus · Pragmatic Alliance
Secunda Geta and Cordus Zoilus are allies on one matter only, agreed in plain terms, and never stretch it to anything else.
Cordus Zoilus has asked Secunda Geta to stretch the alliance to a second matter. Secunda Geta has not said yes or no.

## Identity & Daily Life
Identity & Daily Life
Metropolis
ANCHOR FACTS
Complexity
Diversified — broad institutional economic base
Food
Secure
Magic-dependent
CULTURAL NOTES
Public standing is negotiated through family networks, office, clientage, law, and visible contribution to civic life. Masonry courtyard buildings and tiled roofs gather around public squares and processional streets.
IDENTITY
Name
Ripaorum
Tier
Metropolis
Population
22,219
Terrain
Riverside
Layout
River delta metropolis: island districts, bridge-markets, the great river-gate controlling all upstream trade
Age
334 years
Trade access
River
Founded
a great trade house that outgrew every other settlement in the region
CULTURAL IDENTITY
Latin-inspired
Latin-inspired; people here live in a place built in masonry around a civic square, and run by patronage and law.
Built form
Masonry courtyard buildings and tiled roofs gather around public squares and processional streets.
Civic pattern
Councils, magistrates, patrons, and legal corporations compete through offices, charters, and public works.
Exchange
Contract, warehousing, tolls, and notarial practice support long-distance commerce.
Foodways
Market vendors and shared dining customs make food distribution visibly civic.
Sacred life
Household devotion and civic cult practice overlap in festivals, memorials, and patronage.
Defense
Where a place can pay for them, masonry defenses, watch posts, and road gates are kept as civic infrastructure.
Social texture
Public standing is negotiated through family networks, office, clientage, law, and visible contribution to civic life.
Architecture
arcaded market fronts define the principal square
DAILY LIFE
FOOD BALANCE
Production and need are in balance. The settlement feeds itself, with nothing spare against a bad year.

CHARACTER & ORIGIN
Historical character: History & Founding (17).
Settlement Origin:
Sited at the water's edge, where the river was the only road worth having, and it has been the argument for staying ever since. Everything in the region is bought, sold or decided here.

## Tonight
Tonight
What is urgent
The Grand Merchant Oligarchy's hold on Ripaorum is weaker than it looks from outside.
People to voice
Justa Nasica
First Magnate · Holds the seat
Wants to buy a share in the luxury goods quarter before the season is out.
Secunda Geta
the dean · Senior cleric
Wants to see someone who wronged them lose face in public this season.
What the players test first
Food
Secure. Stores: a granary, about 14 months of grain.
Defence
Massive walls and fortifications, Citadel, Multiple garrisons, Professional city watch, Mercenary quarter, Multiple adventurers' guilds.

## Economics & Trade
Economics & Trade
Moderate. Most of what it sells is healing waters.
Prosperity
Moderate
Complexity
Diversified — broad institutional economic base
Trade
River
It gets about four fifths of what its work could yield.
INCOME SOURCES
Criminal Syndicate Revenue
25%
Guild-run fencing, extortion and black-market trade bring money in here, but it goes to the criminals who run them and the treasury sees none of it.
Market Taxes
20%
District-level duties on specialized goods; a major civic revenue stream across the urban districts.
Guild Licensing
12%
Charter fees, quality inspection levies, and licensing of all trades and crafts.
Financial Services
9%
Civic taxes on banking operations, letters of credit, and financial transaction fees.
Property Rents
8%
Ground rents on civic-owned buildings, stalls, and residential plots within the walls.
River Tolls
8%
Tolls on river traffic, dock fees, and ferry rights.
Court Fees & Fines
4%
Filing fees, fines levied on offenders, and fees for notarial and legal certification services.
Fish & Preserved Produce
3%
The fishery here is fished out; the fish sold now is salted and bought in.
Iron & Metalwork
3%
Iron imported from mining regions and worked locally; this income stream is vulnerable to supply disruption.
Spellcasting Services
3%
A busy market for spell services. Identification, augury, message sending, and contract-grade enchanting bring steady coin.
Wool & Textile Trade
3%
Cloth is woven or finished here.
Apothecary & Alchemy
2%
Remedies and simple preparations, made and sold here: a modest trade.

## Power Structure
Power Structure
Grand Merchant Oligarchy governs, with 6 other powers competing for influence.
PUBLIC LEGITIMACY
## 50
50
Tolerated
STABILITY
Unstable (pervasive organized crime)
GOVERNING
Grand Merchant Oligarchy
Economy
GOVERNANCE
Justa Nasica, the exchange master and First Magnate, holds the seat of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy. The Grand Merchant Oligarchy governs Ripaorum. Ripaorum tolerates the Grand Merchant Oligarchy, and no more. Several merchants have been found murdered this season, and people say the guard is doing nothing about it.
POWER DISTRIBUTION
Grand Merchant Oligarchy 35
Merchant Guilds 19
Thieves' Guild 16
Craft Guilds 11
Arcane Orders 11
Military/Guard 5
The Shield Compact 3
POWERS
Grand Merchant Oligarchy
Economy
Governing
Power 35
Strong
The richest merchant houses of the metropolis sit as its senate, so the houses that control the trade also make the law. The craft guilds are present but outpaced; merchants set prices, craft masters object, and merchants win more often than not. They tolerate the guild's cut because fighting it costs more than paying it, and nobody says so aloud.
Merchant Guilds
Economy
Power 19
Significant
Dominant commercial class; their capital and networks give them leverage even formal institutions must respect. Behind the Grand Merchant Oligarchy by too much to challenge openly, so they get their way through petitions and procedure instead. The craft guilds are present but outpaced; merchants set prices, craft masters object, and merchants win more often than not. They tolerate the guild's cut because fighting it costs more than paying it, and nobody says so aloud.
Thieves' Guild
Criminal
Power 16
Minor
Criminal network operating in shadows; controls illicit trade and profits from the gap between law and enforcement. They are always at the table, but cannot set their own terms. They have more real pull than the military right now: officers on their payroll, and commanders who know better than to ask questions.
Craft Guilds
Economy
Power 11
Minor
The trades regulate their own production and apprenticeships; a reliable secondary presence in civic life. The merchants consistently outvote them on pricing and labour standards, so craft masters now win what they can by adding their own conditions to each deal.
Arcane Orders
Magic
Power 11
Minor
Magical practitioners are consulted but not formally empowered. Their influence is advisory, transactional, and quietly resented.
Military/Guard
Military
Power 5
Suppressed
Undermanned and underfunded; unable to enforce law consistently and aware of it. The guild is stronger than they are. They can only make arrests where it is safe to, and everyone knows it.
The Shield Compact
Military
Power 3
Suppressed
Undermanned and underfunded; unable to enforce law consistently and aware of it.
FOR THE DM ONLY
Who really runs this metropolis
THE RULING THREE OF GRAND MERCHANT OLIGARCHY
Head · Justa Nasica, the exchange master · also first in Merchant Guilds
Justa Nasica heads the Grand Merchant Oligarchy and also holds a full share in the Merchant Guilds, so what the Merchant Guilds want from the Grand Merchant Oligarchy goes straight to the top.
Second · Pompeia Silanus, the senior guild master
Third · Sempronia Corvinus, the luxury merchant
WHO ANSWERS TO WHOM
Thieves' Guild · led by Marcella Gallus, the network's head
handled by Calvus Barba, the house boss (Gambling district)

handled by Junia Pulvillus, the pawnbroker (Pawnbroker)
Tacitus Moderatus, the wharfinger (Docks/port facilities) · brings information and small favours · a silent partnership
handled by Blanda Hostus, the watcher (Listening post)

handled by Terentia Dentatus, the whisper broker (Whisper market)

handled by Servilia Cato, the guildmaster of thieves (Thieves' guild)
Lucretia Plancus, the beast master (Beast trainers) · brings a place for the money and the goods · held over them: a secret
handled by Mamercus Figulus, the network's spider (Underground network)

handled by Manlia Perdix, the nominal owner (Front businesses)

Potter · Hadrian Lateranus · fronts for Thieves' Guild · handled by Pompeia Lateranus
THE HALL
Living with the criminals. Nobody at the head of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy answers to the Thieves' Guild, and the Grand Merchant Oligarchy has not set out to stamp the Thieves' Guild out.
THE BOOSTS
Merchant Guilds · a little stronger than its own members make it
Justa Nasica, the head of the Grand Merchant Oligarchy, also holds a full share in the Merchant Guilds, and that lends the Merchant Guilds weight they would not have on their own.

## Defense & Security
Defense & Security
Well-defended: massive walls and fortifications, held by the multiple garrisons. It leans on magic to hold.
Readiness
Well-defended
Safety
Moderate
Watch to population
0.2
Food resilience
68
GUARD ASSESSMENT
The garrison and city watch patrol on a regular round, so petty theft is often caught and planned crime seldom is. Thieves here know that the court sits and the cells are real. The garrison and city watch keep the gates, but smugglers have ways in beneath the walls. Hired swords back the garrison.
THREAT ASSESSMENT
Each bar shows how ready the settlement is for that threat, as judged at the first survey; Disasters & Famine is re-judged as your campaign goes on. A longer bar is safer.
Beasts & Monsters
Strong
Active frontier. Walls and garrison provide credible deterrence: most creature threats will not press a defended perimeter. Charter hall handles anything above the garrison usual remit. Adequate for the threat level.
Invasion & War
Strong
Walls and professional garrison provide meaningful deterrence against raiding and conventional assault. Not rated for sustained siege without significant supply stockpiles.
Internal Security
Adequate
Internal security: Moderate. Arrest, trial and gaol all work here.
Economic Survival
Strong
Money enough to ride out a long crisis: emergency measures can be paid for, and the garrison kept paid while it lasts.
Disasters & Famine
Strong
Clergy provide basic wound care: better than nothing, worse than a hospital.
Food endurance :
Under siege the outlying fields would be lost; the granary would carry the metropolis for more than a year at full rations, more than two years on half, and what is still harvested would stretch it further.
ARMED FORCES & FORTIFICATIONS
Fortifications
Massive walls and fortifications
Walls within walls: an outer line and an inner line, with gatehouses at every stage.
Citadel
Inner fortress. Last refuge in siege.
Standing forces
Multiple garrisons
Garrisons spread through the quarters; no single barracks could secure a city this size.
Professional city watch
Full-time keepers of order, about one for every hundred people in the city.
Contracted forces
Mercenary quarter
Organised bands of hired blades. Major forces, hundreds to thousands strong.
Monster response (charter)
Multiple adventurers' guilds
Competing organizations.
Arcane defense
Mages' district
Quarter inhabited by arcane practitioners: towers, workshops, libraries, reagent merchants.
Academy of magic
A formal college of magic. A complete curriculum, rooms for research, and a stream of visiting adepts.
Enchanter's shop
Where magic items are crafted.
Scroll scribe
Spell scrolls for sale. The smallest charm costs what a craftsman earns in a month; a greater working, a merchant’s year.
CRIMINAL ARCHITECTURE
Criminal structure · Organized syndicate
Crime here answers to one ring. It keeps the streets quiet because trouble brings the law, lives on protection money and on whatever official it can pay, and anyone who needs something from it can bargain.
Organized guild crime
Magical crime
Smuggling
ACTIVE CRIMINAL OPERATIONS
Smuggling Network
Moves goods around customs and guild charters. Corrupt officials, unofficial landing points, and false manifests.
Thieves' Guild
Controls the criminal hierarchy. Suppresses random crime in exchange for predictable extraction. Deeply embedded in civic life.
Front Businesses
Legitimate-looking operations used to launder criminal revenue and provide cover for illegal activities.
Underground City
An entire secondary economy operating below street level. Beyond enforcement reach without extraordinary effort.
Assassins' Guild
Professional killers for hire. That there is work for them says how politics is done here.
Gambling District
Operates unlicensed gambling. Revenue funds broader criminal network. Attracts debt spirals and desperation crime.
Red Light District
A quarter given over to pleasure: a trade that runs at the edge of the law.
Underworld: Thieves' Guild · Minor
SUPPORTING CAPABILITIES
Arcane Support
Present
Mages' district, Academy of magic. Detection, wards, counterspell.
Strong
Legal Infrastructure
Court + Prison
Full enforcement chain. Arrest, prosecute, detain.
Medical Readiness
Clergy care
Clerical care. Basic wound and disease management.

## Viability Assessment
Viability Assessment
Viable
Viable, as long as the four trade routes it depends on stay open.
VERDICT
Viable
Ripaorum's trades run on metals brought in from elsewhere; if that supply stopped, they would stop with it.
Judged at the first survey; not re-run since.
MAGIC DEPENDENCY
This settlement leans on arcane infrastructure. If magic-supporting institutions are removed or their staff lost, the dependent chains below would fail.
